# YouTube Sources Sub-Index

All ingested YouTube video source pages, grouped by channel, in publication-date order.
**★ = L3-candidate** (flagged for promotion into topics/persona during a synthesis
pass). Pipeline state per video lives in [[../../pipeline/ledger.csv]].

## @thefutur

| 2024-10-01 | ★★★★ [Widen the gap between the give and the ask (short)](2024-10-01-yt-4P4VDBSIrPw.md) | sales-clients; mindset | ✅ **The one short from the 2024 stretch that survived verification** — and it was checked against the influences hub AND the topic hubs before being called new, after four false-absence claims earlier in the run. The give-first **concept** is thoroughly covered (gift economy, *a gift with an expectation is manipulation*, reciprocity, the emotional-debt mechanism); **this FORMULATION was not**: *"**when you give and you ask and there's NO GAP between the two — IT'S CALLED A TRANSACTION. THERE'S NO FEELINGS OF GOODWILL.** I gave you money, you did a service. I give you a resource, I got your email. **It's just like SO FAST**… every time we ask, what we need to do is to **WIDEN THE GAP between the time in which we give and the time in which we ask.**"* ✅ **It turns give-first from a POSTURE into a VARIABLE** — the interval — and explains the hub's existing failure cases: the lead magnet that instantly demands an email, the free consult that becomes a pitch. **Not too-small gifts; gifts with too small a gap.** Promoted to `topics/sales-clients` §123 |
| 2025-05-18 | ★★★★ [Business Books for Entrepreneurs (short)](2025-05-18-yt-YkhN94U6MVE.md) | business; mindset; sales-clients | ⚠️ **A SECOND PROVENANCE READING LIST** (cf. [2026-03-07](2026-03-07-yt-A-LQwz40G7Y.md)). **No new names — three new CHARACTERISATIONS.** ★★★★ **JIM ROHN**, *Seven Strategies for Wealth and Happiness*: *"**THE GODFATHER OF COACHES**… **Jim Rohn has been THE MENTOR FOR EVERYBODY THAT YOU LOOK UP TO TODAY** and the authors you read. **His most notable student or mentee is ANTHONY ROBBINS.**"* ⚠️ A lineage claim about **other people**, recorded as his characterisation. ★★★★★ **MICHAEL BUNGAY STANIER**, *The Coaching Habit* — **upgraded from a single opener to a STRUCTURAL source**: *"an excellent framework for understanding **THE VALUE OF ASKING GREAT QUESTIONS, BEING SLOWER TO ANSWER, AND STAYING CURIOUS A LITTLE BIT LONGER**… and it so happens to **OVERLAP WITH HOW YOU DO SALES**"* ✅ a **named triad the corpus holds only in pieces.** ★★★★ **BLAIR ENNS** — a **SIXTH dated attestation**, and the first to classify the book by category: *"a great **POSITIONING** book for people who **sell expert services**"*, prefaced *"no surprise to anybody who's been following me"* |
| 2025-06-24 | ★★★★★ ["You're too EXPENSIVE" (short)](2025-06-24-yt-TojOt9k3d0c.md) | sales-clients; pricing | ★★★★★ **The corpus is rich on pricing and objections and has NO SCRIPT for the commonest objection of all.** This gives it — and **the move is not a defence of the price**: *"if your prospect says 'that's too expensive', here's what I would say: **'I'M SORRY. I THINK I MISJUDGED THE PROBLEM WE'RE TRYING TO SOLVE. I thought we were trying to solve a really big problem that had an impact on your business…'**"* ✅ **Three things at once**: it **concedes nothing on the number**, it **relocates the disagreement to the PROBLEM** (too-expensive is read as a mis-sized problem, not a mis-set fee), and it **takes the error onto himself** so the client is free to correct him *upward* rather than defend a low figure. ✅ Pairs with *"anytime you can't sell something and it's not worth a lot, **it's because they don't feel the pain**"* ([2025-11-24](2025-11-24-yt-UhklGQya1r4.md)) — **this is that diagnosis turned into a sentence you can say out loud.** ⚠️ Truncated before the script completes; no ending inferred |
| 2025-08-19 | ★★★★ [Every Dollar Is a Vote (short)](2025-08-19-yt-u5Y-nDFCwj4.md) | branding; content-strategy | ★★★★★ **Identity-purchase reasoning extended from TRANSACTIONS to ENGAGEMENT**: *"**every time you're asking someone to watch your content, to subscribe, to comment, to join your email list, or to buy from you — YOU HAVE TO BE ABLE TO GO OUT THERE AND TELL THEM WHAT ELSE THEY'RE BUYING.** It's not just the service… **they're buying their IDENTITY, and they want to find MEANING, because we as humans are MEANING-MAKING MACHINES.**"* ✅ Credits **ANNA LAPPÉ** (already an influence) for *"every time that you spend money, you're **casting a vote for the kind of world you want**."* ⚠️⚠️ **GUARD: contains a DATED (Aug-2025) commercial opinion about a NAMED LIVING CEO and his company — delivered WITH his own disclosure that he owns one of their cars.** Recorded exactly as spoken, with the date and the disclosure attached; **a persona must not restate it as a current view or elaborate the political content** |
| 2025-08-31 | ★★★★★ [2X Your Income As a Solopreneur (short)](2025-08-31-yt-ulPzCD7P3oU.md) | pricing; business | ★★★★★ **A WORKED COSTING METHOD the corpus does not have** — it argues for higher rates constantly and never says **how to find the market rate** or **how to build a number up**. ① *"**find THREE PEOPLE who are AT OR ABOVE YOUR LEVEL**, find out what their rates are… **just average the three together**"* (note the constraint: *at or above*, not peers). ② ★★★★★ **The load-bearing move**: *"**ACT AS IF YOU COULD NOT ACTUALLY DO ANY OF THE WORK YOURSELF** — all you're doing is **managing the client relationships and the project**"* → which prices **the delivery, not your time**. ③ The build-up, with his numbers: **8 days × \$1,000 = \$8,000 + \$3,500 PM = \$11,500 break-even → +30% profit (\$3,450) = \$14,950** — *"**this is NOT where we stop, because this is JUST TO BREAK EVEN. You need to add PROFIT.**" ✅ **File as the COST-PLUS alternative**, consistent with his de-recommendation of value-based pricing for most viewers at [2025-06-12](2025-06-12-yt-JIbsSwvfytI.md) |
| 2025-09-06 | ★★★★ [What You Say Is Who You Are (short)](2025-09-06-yt-ISmzwnxLZ1s.md) | mindset; branding | ⚠️ Truncated both ends. ★★★★★ **An EPISTEMIC argument for language discipline that the non-violent-language thread does not make** — that thread is about the *effect* of your words; **this says your words are all anyone can ever access**: *"you **can't be a kind person if you don't use kind words**. It doesn't happen that way. **WHAT YOU SAY IS WHO YOU ARE.** … **People only experience you based on what comes out of your mouth.** Rarely do they have this whole display of your entire life… **YOUR ENTIRE REPUTATION IS SIMPLY WHAT YOU SAY.**"* ★★★★ And the reframe: *"**when you say 'I don't like this person', what you really mean is: THEY SAY THINGS I DON'T LIKE.**"* ✅ Sits directly under **Bezos's *"what people say when you're not in the room"*** as its mechanism. ⚠️ File **beside** the NVC material, not merged |
| 2025-11-11 | ★★★★ [Embrace Your Imperfect Self! (short)](2025-11-11-yt-2K32HmJSeAM.md) | mindset; content-strategy | ★★★★ **A SECOND AI-MOAT MECHANISM, and it is not the one the corpus records.** Elsewhere the moat is **taste and judgement**; here it is **a condition**: *"the thing I love most about each of you is **YOUR DIRTY IMPERFECT SELF**. And the thing that **AI WILL NEVER REPLACE is THE WEIRD STICKY TENSION that happens in a space like this** — unless you're a cyborg, **THE REAL IN-PERSON EXPERIENCE is what the machine cannot replace.**"* ⚠️ **File beside the taste material, not merged** — it claims something different in kind. ★★★★ And a **self-undercutting close** from his own stage: *"I know a lot of you are **socially awkward, weird, introverts JUST LIKE MYSELF** — go talk to somebody today… **NOT THE PANEL. NOT WHAT YOU HEAR — because you're not going to do anything anyways. GO TALK TO THREE PEOPLE.**"* ✅ He tells the audience the content they came for isn't the valuable part, **including his own panel**, and substitutes a countable action |
| 2025-11-24 | ★★★★ [The #1 Reason Clients Don't Buy (short)](2025-11-24-yt-UhklGQya1r4.md) | sales-clients; pricing | ★★★★★ **Collapses two problems the corpus treats separately into ONE diagnosis**: *"**anytime you can't sell something AND it's not worth a lot, IT'S BECAUSE THEY DON'T FEEL THE PAIN**"* — so *can't sell it* and *can't charge much for it* are **the same symptom with one cause**, not separate failures of positioning, confidence or pricing method. The qualifier: *"we need to find someone who has **A BURNING URGENT DESIRE to solve a problem they've felt MASSIVE PAIN AROUND**."* Set up by the proverbial *"**if the only tool you have is a hammer, all the problems look like nails**"* — ⚠️ which **he does not attribute** (*"there's an expression"*), and the hedge is preserved. ⚠️⚠️ **The transcript ENDS MID-SENTENCE** (*"you should help them to find the…"*) and **no completion is inferred** |
| 2026-03-07 | ★★★★★ [3 Marketing Books to Grow Your Business (short)](2026-03-07-yt-A-LQwz40G7Y.md) | branding; content-strategy; business | ⚠️⚠️ **A PROVENANCE SOURCE — and an `entities/influences` job.** ★★★★★ **The strongest statement of intellectual debt in the shorts corpus**: of Seth Godin — *"**it's got to be THE GOAT**"* — and of ***PERMISSION MARKETING***, *"**WHICH I BASE A LOT OF THE WAY I TEACH ABOUT BRANDING AND MARKETING FROM THIS BOOK ITSELF**"* ✅ **the corpus currently records Godin only for *The Dip*; this upgrades him from cited to FOUNDATIONAL**, and adds ***This is Marketing***. ⚠️★★★★ **AL RIES and JACK TROUT, *POSITIONING*, are ENTIRELY ABSENT from the corpus** despite positioning being central to his teaching — *"a classic… it really helps you understand marketing on a broader perspective."* ★★★ **Allan Dib**, *The 1-Page Marketing Plan*, with a new use-case framing: *"if you really need a go-to-market strategy and **you don't want to deal with the philosophy and all that stuff**, you would probably just go straight to this book."* ✅ Note **the implicit hierarchy**: Ries/Trout for **perspective**, Godin for **foundation**, Dib for **execution** |

| 2015-09-23 | ★★ [AMA w/ Keir McLaren Pt 2: Adapting to a Changing Creative Economy](2015-09-23-yt-8CWD3KSaeVs.md) | business | ⚠️⚠️ **GUEST-DOMINATED — attribution critical.** Part 2 of the **2015 AMA series that Kier McLaren dominates**, which his entity page already flags by name; **the great majority of the speech is his** and none of it trains the persona. Chris co-hosts with **Jose Caballer** and mostly frames and prompts. ⏳ **The oldest source in this run** — pre-rebrand, when the show was still called *The Process* — and a clean dating marker. Kier's argument (industries that fail to see their own displacement, illustrated with buffalo on the plains and the carriage makers who *"**none of them made it through, because none of them believed it would happen**"*) is fenced as context and already lives on his entity page. ✅ **Worth noting, not promoting**: it is the same shape as Chris's own 2026 argument (*"every 3 to 5 years your industry is going to be disrupted"*) — **but he never credits Kier for it, so the connection is the curator's observation, not an attribution.** Chris's yield is framing only, plus a looser co-hosted on-camera register consistent with his composed style having been trained later |

| 2016-02-13 | ★★★★★ [Win The Interview — Job Interview Tips](2016-02-13-yt-pKMfPGAZHYI.md) | sales-clients; business; mindset | ★★★★★ **A five-tip interview clinic that is really a QUESTIONING-AND-LISTENING clinic — and it moves two frameworks the corpus dates much later back to FEBRUARY 2016.** ★★★★★ **The magic question**, *"the holy grail of questions"*: *"**if your deity of choice gave you THE PERFECT DESIGNER to fill this role — what would that look like? And let them describe the profile of that person**"* → and the reason for the timing, which is the whole technique: *"**ask that at the BEGINNING, because you can then TAILOR what you're telling them to what they said to you. YOU'RE READING IT BACK TO THEM**"* → *"that works in dating too."* ★★★★★ **PRECEDENCE — "the value of you as a person is reflected in the QUALITY OF THE QUESTIONS YOU ASK"**, his own 2016 statement of an idea the wiki currently carries via the 2025–26 Demartini epigraph; *"if you ask 'what time do people show up' — that's not a high-value question."* ★★★★★ **PRECEDENCE — "silence the thoughts in your head and PLAY A GAME: WHOEVER TALKS FIRST LOSES"** — the *then-shut-up* rule a YEAR BEFORE [2017-02-04](2017-02-04-yt-swiG31QGKVc.md), phrased as a game. Plus ★★★★ **gauge engagement and stop** (*"if you see no interest — please, PLEASE SAVE YOURSELF… you're boring people"*), ★★★★ **permission to punt** (*"that's a great question, I'd like to follow up with you later — THAT BEATS ANYTHING YOU'RE GOING TO MAKE UP ON THE SPOT. Do what they do in football and PUNT"*), and *"the fact that you got an interview means that you're qualified."* ★★★★★ **Closes with a LIVE ROLEPLAY** in which Chris plays the creative director, states the real 2016 Blind pivot in role (*"**out of being a motion design firm exclusively, to being THE DIGITAL AGENCY OF THE FUTURE**"* — corroborating [2016-03-04](2016-03-04-yt-KlnOS_Blig4.md)), says what he actually hires for (*"**driven, a SELF-STARTER, takes initiative — not somebody waiting to be told what the next step is**"*), and then **breaks frame to catch himself**: *"**I'm stopping myself from talking right there — because in my head I'm saying: THAT'S ME, DUDE.**" ⚠️ Co-hosted with Jose Caballer; the orange-jumpsuit story is his |

| 2016-03-04 | ★★★★★ [How did you start your design business? AMA w/ Chris Do](2016-03-04-yt-KlnOS_Blig4.md) | business; sales-clients; design-craft; mindset | ★★★★★ **BIOGRAPHY LANDMARK — the richest biographical source in this run, and the fullest Blind founding story anywhere.** ★★★★★ **The founding, with new specifics**: *"**Blind was started and formed in DECEMBER OF 1995 as a corporation**"*; he'd worked at **an ad agency and a punk rock music label** and *"**didn't feel like I could express who I was**"*; then ★★★★★ **his UNCLE called** — *"**do you want to start a design company?**"* — because an attorney partner's client owned hotels and *"**it's cheaper to own one than to continually pass the work out.**"* He'd been out of school *"**no more than two and a half months.**"* ★★★★★ **The business plan, pre-Google**: *"**search was not even a thing yet**"* → he phoned his brother in Silicon Valley to ask *"what is the internet?"*, then a roommate's investment-banker father → *"**use the resources that you know. Use the resources that are around you.**"* ★★★★★ **The ask and the answer**: *"**I just came up with a wild number, and the number was \$100,000. Completely arbitrary**"* — though the reasoning was **six months of RUNWAY** — and *"**he reaches into his briefcase and writes a check on the spot. Not for \$100,000**… **he wrote a check for \$5,000. 5%**… **No contracts. No percentage.**"* ★★★★ **The first office**: the Brewery in downtown LA, 2,000 sq ft — *"**I literally slept upstairs, walked down the metal stairway, and the office was there.**"* When he quit his freelance gig: *"**call us back when you fail**"* → *"**there's no way I could fail. Let's just go for it.**"* ★★★★★ **The breakthrough moment, and why he had an answer ready**: *"**I kept in my mind: maybe one day somebody's going to ask me that question. I NEED TO KNOW WHEN THAT MOMENT IS**"* → **the first national TV spot, about a year in.** ★★★★★ ***The Great Brain*** **as a childhood template** — a book about three brothers, narrated by the youngest about the schemer: *"**that's a role model for me**"* (he is himself the middle of three). ★★★★★ **THE EARLIEST MECHANIC PARABLE**, with the pricing move later tellings drop: *"**whatever you're going to say, I'm going to say the opposite**… **you're an ORDER TAKER. You don't think. YOU DON'T DIAGNOSE**"* → the medical version (*"let's operate — when it's just a tummy ache"*) → and ★★★★★ *"**I don't do diagnostics for free.** I'm going to charge you \$60 — **and that will be a CREDIT applied to whatever you want to do. But you don't need to do it with me.**"* → *"**he's an expert. I came here to hire an expert.**"* ★★★★★ **Order taker → consultant**, dated to 2016: *"**we are positioning ourselves as BUSINESS DESIGN CONSULTANTS**… **I don't prescribe solutions until I understand the problem. I'M NO LONGER AN ORDER TAKER**"* — with the honesty clause *"**I cannot guarantee you results… that's YOUR decision.**"* Plus ★★★★★ **giving with a stated limit** (*"**I try to give as much of myself as I can without hurting myself**… **what I give away is SLEEP**"*), **self-selection as "law of attraction"**, and ★★★★ *"**creative types tend to not value their own creative time. So guess what — they don't value your time either.**"* ⚠️ Contains a dated 2016 industry read, recorded as a prediction |

| 2017-02-04 | ★★★★★ [How Do I Get More Work & Scale My Design Business?](2017-02-04-yt-swiG31QGKVc.md) | pricing; sales-clients; business | ★★★★★ **A pricing masterclass delivered as coaching, and almost certainly the EARLIEST RECORDED PRICE BRACKETING.** *"**You're going to bracket a price. I don't want you to say 10 anymore — I want you to say BETWEEN 10 TO 20**… **so 10 looks like a smaller number next to 20**"* → and if challenged, *"**well, I don't know what you need yet, and this is what I charge.**"* ★★★★★ **Then shut up** — *"**I don't want you to quick-pivot, because that says: I'm not totally comfortable with that number just yet. JUST LET THEM BE UNCOMFORTABLE WITH THE SILENCE, and you just sit there and chill**"*; *"it's kind of like **the law from God. I just read it off the tablet.**"* ★★★★★ **And the sharpest argument in the corpus for why the silence matters**: *"**if I ask you for a price and you say 'oh, it's this' — and then you TALK — I just get the feeling you're not confident about the number. THAT'S WHEN I COME IN, SAY MY NUMBER, AND I'LL GET IT FOR MORE THAN YOU.**"* With his own discomfort admitted (*"**I'm not really that comfortable saying the numbers all the time**… **it is hard sometimes to ask for money**"*) against the belief that carries him (*"**in my heart of hearts, whatever I charge, it's worth way more than what I charge**"*). ★★★★★ **His own brackets, disclosed and escalating**: *"**30 to 50 — and soon it'll be 30 to 80, then 50 to 100. I'm just going to KEEP BUMPING OUT THE BRACKETS, because the more of this we do, the more PROOF I have**"* — the number rises with **evidence**, not nerve. Plus the verbatim discovery script (*"and then I shut up"*), travel as extra, *"**perfect the model**"*, ★★★★ *"**one of the things that can kill you more than anything else is to become distracted WHILE YOUR GOLD MINE COULD STILL BE RIGHT IN FRONT OF YOU**"*, runway **for the whole team**, a qualified view of retainers, and ★★★★★ **his own account of his core skill**: *"**I'm a good listener and I can synthesise information. I can repackage it so people can HEAR THEIR OWN THOUGHTS SAID MORE CLEARLY THAN THEY COULD HAVE SAID IT.**"* ⏳ Also a marker of where he was pre-consulting: *"**how you get there, I don't know. I'M WORKING ON IT MYSELF.**"* |

| 2017-03-27 | ★★★★★ [How do you balance work and life? — Otis College](2017-03-27-yt-BX5Jy0OIVSg.md) | mindset; business; content-strategy | ★★★★★ **The clearest statement of WORK–LIFE INTEGRATION in the corpus — and he tells it AGAINST HIS OWN PAST.** *"**I don't believe in work-life balance, and I don't believe everything in moderation. EVERYTHING IN MODERATION LEADS TO A MEDIOCRE LIFE**… **you have to go intense and you have to go extreme**"* (fenced immediately: *"not the gambling… I'm talking about YOUR PASSION, YOUR ART"*). ★★★★★ **The four quadrants** — work / passion / play / the rest of your life — *"**there's no limit as to how big the circles can get… the stuff in the middle is the stuff that makes you really happy. AND IT TURNS OUT, IT'S MY BELIEF, THAT THAT'S WHAT MAKES YOU REALLY VALUABLE TOO**"*; the failure mode is *"**we might make money, but WE RUIN OUR RELATIONSHIPS.**"* ★★★★★ **THE REVERSAL, dated**: *"**I used to just run Blind and that's all I was doing — so it was ALL ABOUT WORK. DON'T CALL ME AT HOME, I don't respond to your emails**… **I try not to let those two worlds bleed. AND IT CREATES ALL SORTS OF PROBLEMS.**"* ⏳ **A falsifiable March-2017 prediction**: The Futur *"**is all the things I love, all combined together at the same time… I believe it'll become like a \$100 MILLION COMPANY**"*; *"it's taking me a while — **I'm in my 40s.**" ★★★★★ **The only concrete picture of how the company was RUN in 2017**: 10am–7pm, loose structure, and *"**a sign of a good leader is somebody who TELLS YOU WHAT THE GOAL IS but LEAVES YOU ALONE to figure out how to get to the goal**"* — with the micromanagement failure described as a mechanism (*"**every time he's thinking WHAT DOES HE WANT, and he stops thinking about what he's supposed to do. THE TALENT THAT BROUGHT HIM IN THE DOOR IS NOW GETTING CLAMPED DOWN**"*), the 3-day commitment protocol (*"**no babysitting, no helicoptering**"*), the redirect that is still questions (*"**I'll ask you lots of questions, hopefully to recharge you and redirect you**"*), **Daniel Pink on autonomy**, and *"**all I want to do is put the GUIDELINES ON THE FIELD — and within that, it's VAST AREAS FOR YOU TO EXPLORE.**" ★★★★★ **Closes with a rapid-fire hobby list — the densest character material in the corpus**: *"**Marvel over DC**"*, skateboarding *"part of my past"*, volleyball, **UFC from "UFC number one"** (*"**Dana White, I've sent you a lot of money, my friend**"*), fishing (*"**I started thinking about ORDERING A KIT TO RAISE MY OWN EARTHWORMS**"*), and ★★★★★ **the self-diagnosis it illustrates**: *"**I'M A DEEP DIVER. Whatever I do, I do with ALL OF MY HEART — and then I'm bored and I move on to the next thing.**"* Plus ★★★★★ **the collecting ethic** (*"**I'm that guy at the bookstore — I ACTUALLY BUY IT**… **I believe in buying things to SUPPORT THE PEOPLE WHO CREATE THOSE THINGS**"*) and ★★★★★ **the Art Center teaching rule**: *"**you cannot teach until FIVE YEARS after you've graduated**"* — because *"**if you're an amazing instructor, what's the highest percentage of knowledge I can extract from you? 70%**… a student who graduates and teaches that course again — **YOU'VE ADDED NOTHING. I can never be better than my teacher in that one discipline. But if I go out and learn architecture, interior design, business — I smash those — then MAYBE ALL MY PARTS ARE MORE THAN THE TEACHER.**"* ⚠️ **Family passages summarised, never quoted, per the SUBJECT.md privacy rule** |

| 2017-05-03 | ★★★ [Blair Enns Interview — author of 'The Win Without Pitching Manifesto'](2017-05-03-yt-KtL02T5MonA.md) | pricing; sales-clients; business | ⚠️ **GUEST-DOMINATED — Blair Enns is the interviewee and speaks for most of the episode; his frameworks are NOT persona data.** But the Chris-attributed material is biography-grade. ★★★★★ **He sources one of his own signature client lines, against himself**: at a Paley award-show panel he answered "what do you do when clients prescribe solutions to you" with *"**you're probably right — but let's make sure**"*, and afterwards a fellow panellist said *"they don't teach this stuff in school, it's not like you could read it in a book, right Chris?"* — *"**and I SMILED A LITTLE GUILTY SMILE and I said: well, actually, I did read it in a book.**"* → *"**one of the seminal books that have changed the way I do business — and if you think I'm really smooth, YOU'VE GOT TO GO TO THE SOURCE.**"* ★★★★★ **A named contributing cause of leaving commercial motion design**: pitching was *"kind of the COST OF ENTRY"*, so *"**there's no way this book can tell me I can't pitch**"* — *"**with some kind of RESISTANCE I read the book — AND IT REALLY TRANSFORMED MY THINKING**… **it's applicable to everything else I'm doing. AND I WANT TO GET OUT OF THAT INDUSTRY. So that began to spark.**"* (⚠️ keep the *"began to spark"* hedge). ★★★★ **How he actually reads**: *"**admittedly I'm NOT A PROLIFIC READER — I sit down and read bits, then I get distracted, then I lose my place, and I start over**… **it sits on my shelf for a while**"* — ✅ which **sharpens rather than contradicts** the book-buying at [2017-03-27](2017-03-27-yt-BX5Jy0OIVSg.md): he buys prolifically, he does not read prolifically. Plus ★★★★★ his own pricing rule — *"**my trick is always to charge AS MUCH MONEY AS THE CLIENT IS WILLING TO PAY — AND MORE**"* — ★★★★ *"**the nature of the creative person is to try to GIVE MORE OF THEMSELVES, TO OVER-DELIVER**"*, ✅ **salmon fishing** confirmed, and a dated *"I have ideas for books but I've NOT PASSED THE TIPPING POINT."* |

| 2017-06-08 | ★★★★★ [Motion Design Graduate Portfolio Review](2017-06-08-yt-5MVuviyGW0c.md) | design-craft; business; mindset | ★★★★★ **A short, blunt live critique with the highest yield-per-minute in this run.** ★★★★★ **The distinction the whole review turns on**: *"**when I ask 'what is this for', I don't mean the tools and techniques you used**… **THIS IS THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN STUDENT WORK AND PROFESSIONAL WORK: PROFESSIONAL WORK HAS AN APPLICATION. IT HAS A PURPOSE. It's not just about testing a piece of software or trying a technique.** Make your work feel like there's an application — **LIKE I'VE ALREADY SEEN IT ON TV.**"* ★★★★★ **The craft test**: *"**I should be able to PARK THE PLAYHEAD AT ANY POINT and find me a good frame that I can print out and it looks like a beautiful poster.**"* ★★★★★ **And the corpus's sharpest argument for picking a lane by its COMPARISON SET, not by taste**: *"**why not just do a 2D paper-animated [version]? THAT'S WHERE YOU SHOULD PUT YOUR CREATIVITY** — and not trying to render 3D mountains. Because **what are you going to do with 3D mountains — you're going to work for PIXAR or ILM? No, you're not. MAKING IT REAL HURTS YOU** — because **we have *Life of Pi*, and then we have [your reel].**"* Plus ★★★★★ **the pre-framing script** for presenting a reel (context, your role, the problem, the duration → *"now I know how long it is, I know what I'm looking for, I KNOW HOW TO JUDGE THE WORK"*) — ✅ the same tailor-before-you-present move as the magic question at [2016-02-13](2016-02-13-yt-pKMfPGAZHYI.md); ★★★★ type as an amateur tell (*"**you get to pick the background, you get to pick the typeface — SO WHY WOULD YOU PICK SOMETHING THAT MAKES IT HARD TO READ?**"*); the directness in live use (*"**it's jacked up. Okay — I'm just going to say it like it is**"*); and ★★★★★ the closing rule: *"**THE SUREST WAY TO GET WORK IS NOT TO DO WORK THAT LOOKS LIKE STUDENT WORK. DO WORK THAT LOOKS LIKE IT WOULD FIT ON THE COMPANY'S REEL.**"* ✅ The student, unprompted: *"he was very serious and up front — **he wasn't passive at all, and I really appreciated that.**" |

| 2017-09-27 | ★★★★★ [How To Structure A Proposal For Web & Branding Projects (ep.3 w/ Rebecca)](2017-09-27-yt-UHrjn11F6hc.md) | sales-clients; pricing; business | ★★★★★ **LANDMARK — one of the strongest sales sources in the corpus, and the home of two of the wiki's most valuable attributions.** ★★★★★ **THE FOUR THINGS**, elicited from the coachee and then answered point by point as a complete first-meeting method: **COMFORTABLE** (*"how do I make you feel comfortable? **WELL, I'M NOT IN A RUSH**"*), **KNOWLEDGEABLE** (*"**I demonstrate my knowledge BY BEING A GOOD LISTENER** — and **I CAN'T ADVISE YOU UNTIL I HEAR WHAT THE PROBLEMS MAY BE**"*), **RAPPORT** (*"**if you're a slow, smooth-talking person, I want to be slow and smooth-talking; if you're talking really fast, I need to talk really fast**… **I'm going to change my TEMPO, my TONE, my LANGUAGE, my BODY LANGUAGE — everything — TO MATCH AND MIRROR how you're behaving**… **I just want to SYNC UP to you**"*) and **EASY** (*"**I want them to ENVISION WORKING WITH ME — that this is how it's going to be THE ENTIRE ENGAGEMENT**"*). ★★★★★ **THE ORIGIN OF "JUST ASK", CREDITED BY NAME** — and the confession it resolves: *"**that's a game that I USED TO PLAY. I used to — believe it or not — GO INTO CONFERENCE CALLS FOR SIX-FIGURE JOBS AND NOT ASK THE CLIENT WHAT THEY WANTED US TO BUILD**"* → *"**this is something I learned from MY BUSINESS COACH, KIER MCLAREN. He's the one who said: 'KIDDO, WHY DON'T YOU JUST ASK? Just be yourself and just ask.' As soon as he said that, IT WAS LIKE THE HEAVENS PARTED and the sun came through and shone in my face. And it was like: THAT SEEMS SO OBVIOUS AND SIMPLE — THEN WHY THE HECK HADN'T I DONE THIS?**"* → *"**we walk around with an OPERATING SYSTEM that is incorrect. It's flawed — and most of the time IT'S US WRITING THE CODE. So that's the good news: BECAUSE WE CAN REWRITE THE CODE.**"* ★★★★★ **The estimating method**: *"**it's IMPOSSIBLE TO BID A PROJECT THAT YOU DON'T KNOW THE SCOPE OF WORK for — you're only making a CRAZY GUESS**"* (a website is *"like building a house — no two are the same"*), so **charge for the strategy that produces the scope** — *"**or IF WE EVEN NEED TO BUILD A WEBSITE IN THE FIRST PLACE. I NEVER MAKE THAT ASSUMPTION**"* — and meanwhile give **a range explicitly labelled as ASSUMPTIONS** (*"my best guess — and this is a best guess as it is… **I don't know, DON'T HOLD ME TO IT**"*). ★★★★★ **The paid-estimate script, credited verbatim to Blair Enns** (*"**not only do you need to READ it — you need to MEMORISE it, PRACTISE it, BE ABLE TO TEACH IT**"*): *"**I'm not in the business of building proposals that cost time and money**… **you can sit down with me, OR you can PAY ME A THOUSAND DOLLARS and I'll build you an estimate. But I NEED TO BE PAID FOR MY TIME. I CAN'T GET THAT BACK**"* — with **Cialdini's consistency principle** as the reason to prefer the meeting: *"**we have LEVELS OF INCREASING COMMITMENT from you**… **so I'm BRINGING YOU IN.**"* ★★★★★ **Qualifying**: *"**I'm going to GET TO THE NUMBER REALLY FAST — as fast as it seems not rude to do**"*; *"**DON'T GET EXCITED OVER THE PROSPECT OF DOING WORK THAT YOU FORGET TO ASK ABOUT THE BUDGET**"*; budget/timeline/creative fit/personality fit; and the written filter — *"**we WRITE PROTOCOL after every one of those and say: how do we prevent this from happening? So WE TIGHTEN THE FILTER**"* → *"**LISTEN TO YOUR GUT REGARDLESS OF HOW MUCH YOU THINK YOU NEED THE MONEY**… **I WILL PAY YOU MONEY TO LEAVE.**"* ★★★★★ **90% of the proposal is already done** — *"**we didn't change who we were**… what we're changing is the cover letter, the budget and the timeline."* ⚠️ **Also records, as stated, a candid competitive tactic**: the car-repair parable is deployed deliberately — *"**that's why I PLANT THE SEEDS OF DOUBT**… **but I DON'T SAY THOSE WORDS. I just plant the seeds, subtly**… **I'm trying to DIFFERENTIATE the whole time.**"* ✅ Ep.4 of this series is [2017-10-03](2017-10-03-yt--en6iRuZ9DM.md) |

| 2018-06-13 | ★★★★ [Designers React to Design Critique — Young Guns Ep.6](2018-06-13-yt-FsyJodhEze0.md) | design-craft; sales-clients; business | ⚠️ **PARTICIPANT-DOMINATED** — the runtime is the Young Guns reacting to a critique Chris gave in Ep.5; Chris speaks only in the framing and the close. **But the close is a landmark.** ★★★★★ **THE ONLY BEHAVIOURAL EVIDENCE IN THE CORPUS OF BLAIR ENNS'S INFLUENCE**: *"**I wouldn't be me if I didn't throw you a curveball. This is *The Win Without Pitching Manifesto*, written by Blair Enns. INSTEAD OF DOING A DESIGN CHALLENGE, I WANT YOU TO READ THIS BOOK**… chock full of wisdom about **how to POSITION YOURSELF, how to BECOME AN EXPERT, HOW TO SELL WITHOUT SELLING**… **TOP TO BOTTOM, FRONT TO BACK. LEARN EVERYTHING IN THIS BOOK AND APPLY IT TO YOUR LIFE.** How am I going to know you actually read it? **Because for the very next challenge WE ARE GOING TO DO A GAME SHOW — LIVE ON AIR — I'm going to TEST how many of these concepts you've mastered. WINNER TAKES ALL.**"* ✅ **He interrupts a DESIGN mentorship to make it read a SALES book and builds an exam around it** — and it cashes the standard he set nine months earlier at [2017-09-27](2017-09-27-yt-UHrjn11F6hc.md) (*"you need to memorise it, practise it, be able to teach it"*), now enforced on other people. ★★★★ **He also devotes a whole episode to the recipients critiquing HIS critique** — *"**did you feel it was fair? Did you feel it was honest? WAS THAT TOO HARD, OR TOO EASY ON YOU?**"* — ✅ **and publishes the disagreement rather than editing it out** (one participant: *"I'm not sure if I agree with Chris that I should have used the negative space"*; another argues the user journey in their country makes his premise wrong). ★★★★ The close, a hedged apology that doesn't withdraw anything: *"**I'm glad you guys have the FORTITUDE to take the lessons and the criticisms I've shared with you. I'm sorry — but I believe this is all on our path to growth, and SOMETIMES THESE THINGS STING A LITTLE BIT.**"* ★★★ Plus a documented critique technique, reported by a participant: **he puts TRACING PAPER over your design and draws on it** |

| 2019-04-03 | ★★★★ [Designers React to Critique of Their Work — Young Guns Ep.11](2019-04-03-yt-qIvhzoCHzpA.md) | design-craft; branding; sales-clients | ★★★★ **A dense critique source — unlike [Ep.6](2018-06-13-yt-FsyJodhEze0.md), this one intercuts Chris's critique with the reactions, so there is substantial Chris material.** ★★★★★ **REBRAND AS EVOLUTION, and it is a SELLING argument in craft clothing**: praising a step-by-step morph from the old mark to the new — *"**it's often very difficult for existing brands to see the connection between WHERE THEY ARE — the legacy and the equity they've built — and WHERE YOU'RE TAKING THEM.** Showing them this very thorough, logical, step-by-step process, **SO IT DOESN'T SEEM AS FOREIGN TO THEM. IT COMES FROM SOMETHING**"* → *"**I think Mr PAUL RAND would be proud of you — because THIS IS HOW HE WOULD PRESENT HIS LOGOS.**"* ✅ Belongs beside *clients choose the least risky option*. ★★★★★ **The internal-consistency test**, and the sharpest passage: *"**you put a gentleman in here in a business suit** — he looks older, travels well, has good taste — **AND THEN EVERYTHING IN IT DOES NOT FIT WITH HIM. It feels very pastel. IT FEELS LIKE IT COULD BE A WELLNESS SPA, a MAKEUP BRAND**"* → *"**my first impression is — I'm sorry — kind of a MALL AD FOR A MASSAGE PARLOR. 'You have 15 minutes? I'm going to give you a little back rub.'**"* ★★★★ **Typeface FITNESS vs typeface QUALITY**, separated explicitly (*"normally I would say bravo, bravo, excellent typeface… [but] it feels so foreign to German design… **it's too delicate, it's too fine**"*). ★★★★ **Praise is itemised, not generic** (the 3D extruded metal letterform: *"I just can't believe it's Photoshop… the metallic finish, the reflections, the extrusion, the beveled edges"*). ★★★★ On a self-limited brief: *"**you were given the opportunity to design a PREMIUM CLASS airline and you chose one that's SUPER BUDGET**… I would have picked one where **I can go HOG WILD.**"* ✅ **Second and third attested instances of him PUBLISHING DISAGREEMENT with his own critique** — a participant: *"while I agree with the sentiment, **I'm going to have to disagree with him** about turning that mark into an N"*, and again on hierarchy grounds. ★★★★ And care with the people who lost: *"**it just came down to a few small things**… hang in there guys, **you can hold your head high — you all did great work**"*, plus *"**do you think somebody else should have won? Do you want to make a different case for it?**" |

| 2020-08-11 | ★★★★ [Present ONE Logo to Clients. That's it.](2020-08-11-yt-UidUHRMIPyo.md) | design-craft; sales-clients; branding | ⚠️ **@TheFuturAcademy — the FIRST ingest from that channel** (11 of the previous 11 rows were closed as Chris-absent or unverifiable), and ⚠️ **attributed on INTERNAL EVIDENCE, not register**: first-person Blind-era practice, *"now I'm in my 40s"*, and the stylescape vocabulary. **A weaker basis than a self-ID, and flagged as such.** ★★★★ **A then-versus-now piece, and the value is that he describes his own SUPERSEDED practice first**: *"**when I was younger, sometimes we'd literally walk into a client meeting and have 16 LOGOS.** We printed them out, **mounted them on FOAM CORE** and cut them into 4-inch squares, and laid them out in a **NEAT GRID** on the table… **they could take the ones they don't like and MOVE THEM OUT TO THE SIDE — and then we reduce it down to one.**"* → and the honest diagnosis: *"**I didn't know what was right or wrong in terms of what was right FOR THE COMPANY. I knew that the form it took AESTHETICALLY was right**"* — ✅ **volume was standing in for a judgement he could not yet make.** ★★★★★ **What replaced it, and why ONE logo is presentable at all**: discovery → **who the end customer is, their pain points** → **very complex mood boards — "we call them STYLESCAPES, or EMPATHY MAPS"** → *"the ideal customer **looks like this, speaks like this, dresses like this, and lives in this world**… **AND WE GET ALIGNMENT AND AGREEMENT ON THAT**"* → then the logo is a **FIT TEST**: *"find a logo that **FITS NEATLY INTO THIS WORLD** of their ideal customer"*, tested by dropping it into the brand's own imagery — *"**and if it fits, it feels right.**" ★★★★ **The reframe that makes it work**: *"**instead of saying 'oh my god, that's incredible', their expression should be: OF COURSE. YOU DID EXACTLY WHAT WE'D HOPED YOU'D DO**"* — ✅ **a presentation is CONFIRMATION of an agreed brief, not a reveal** |

There's nothing to be gained and everything to lose.**"* **The six steps**: prospecting · **qualifying** (★★★★★ *"can they afford it · do they have **the power** to make decisions · do they have **the desire** to buy"*) · **approach** (*"**what does a home run look like to you?**"*, and ★★★★★ *"**seek to understand, NOT TO DIAGNOSE**"*) · **presentation** (FAB; *"**based on our last conversation, you said…**"*) · ★★★★★ **objection** — *"**don't be disheartened: RAISING OBJECTIONS IS A SIGN OF THE CUSTOMER'S INTEREST. Otherwise they'd just politely nod and move on**"* → *"**take notes**"* · **close** — *"**you must ask for the sale**"*, three buying signals, and ★★★★★ **the hypothetical close**: *"**why a question? IT REDUCES THE PRESSURE of not having to make a decision right now.**"* ★★★★★ **And the Coldplay story**: reviewing the *Ghost Stories* interactive video, Chris Martin waves them off — *"**I love it**"* — and the executive producer asks *"do you want to see the rest?"* → *"**I jumped in to PULL THE EMERGENCY HANDBRAKE**"* → with a generous diagnosis: *"**he was IN LOVE WITH THE PLAN**… **the pitfall of over-preparing gives you tunnel vision**… **he couldn't hear that the client was happy**"* → *"**how much more of a yes could we get?**"* ⏳ Establishes **precedence** for several 2025–26 principles |

| 2021-03-23 | ★★★★★ [Beeple NFT art makes history — selling for millions at auction](2021-03-23-yt-QoMPxUWMo8g.md) | mindset; business; design-craft | ⏳⚠️ **A LANDMARK DATED POSITION (March 2021, the Christie's moment) — never present as a current view.** ★★★★★ **The museum argument**: *"anybody can take a picture of the Mona Lisa, but **that doesn't mean you own the Mona Lisa**"* → we see *"a TRANSLATION of PIGMENT ON CANVAS… **and that's why museums exist. MUSEUMS WOULDN'T EXIST IF THE FACSIMILE OF IT WAS AS POWERFUL AS THE THING ITSELF**"* → **the frame argument** (*"the wall that it's on, the hall that it's in, the venue that it resides in — CONTRIBUTES TO THE ENTIRE EXPERIENCE"*) → ✅ **and he TESTS IT AGAINST THE LOUVRE AND CONCEDES WHERE IT FAILS** (*"eight inches of glass… a sea of people… I don't even know what's behind all that"*) before giving the version that survives: *"in close proximity to a **ROTHKO** or a **WARHOL** you can see **the undulations, the unevenness, the discoloration — THAT'S THE REAL VERSION**… **the SHARED ENERGY between myself and the wall and the painting**"* → ★★★★★ **the crux: *"when you talk about NFTs — THE FACSIMILE IS THE THING."*** ★★★★★ **The Picasso test** — what ownership is for: *"**the artist's hand in the work**… if it sells for 60 million that's just value I had. But **EVERY DAY THAT I GET TO SEE THIS THING IT MAKES ME SMILE**"* vs. the digital case: *"**what are we enjoying? What are we seeing?**… **is it more impressive because it's on my phone on the wall? I DON'T THINK SO.**"* ⚠️★★★★★ **The Ponzi concern, HEDGED and then worked through**: *"**my concern — AND I COULD BE TOTALLY WRONG ON THIS** — is it's like a Ponzi scheme… **ultimately the last person is the one who picks up the tab**"* — grounded in **his own comic collecting**: *"I've seen this before — **the COMICS WORLD IN THE 90s**, printing millions of copies **which makes it NOT RARE AT ALL**, foil covers, alternate covers… **SPECULATORS came in — people who had NO INTEREST IN COMIC BOOKS**… **and it ALMOST RUINED THE ENTIRE COMIC INDUSTRY.**"* ✅ cross-links to the collecting disclosed at [2017-03-27](2017-03-27-yt-BX5Jy0OIVSg.md). ★★★★★ **The verdict splits by ROLE, not technology**: *"**artists should TOTALLY care**… if you're the CREATOR you're FIRST IN… **I JUST WORRY ABOUT THE INVESTORS**"*, closing honestly with *"**I don't really know what's going to happen with this.**" ★★★★★ **"I know when I'm out of my league"** — he hands the technical explainer to a colleague rather than fake it (a dated instance of know-your-depth). ★★★★★ And on Beeple: *"**he is who he presents himself to be, and he's not changed one bit**"*; *"not all of them are winners — **but for the volume, it's incredible**"*; **"WHAT HAVE YOU DONE IN YOUR LIFE THAT YOU WERE THAT CONSISTENT — BESIDES BREATHING?"** |

| 2021-03-18 | ★★★★★ [3 Designers Pitch A Sales Expert–Results Are Hilarious](2021-03-18-yt-zMVayexyVlU.md) | sales-clients; communication | ★★★★★ **LANDMARK — he demonstrates his own method live against a professional sales trainer, and wins.** Two designers pitch **Blair Enns** cold and get deflected. ★★★★★ **His critique names two mistakes**: *"**your first move was to TELL HIM WHAT YOU THINK HIS PROBLEM IS, without even having real dialogue**"* and *"**in your panic, YOU START TALKING ABOUT YOURSELF — and that's when MY INTEREST METER WENT FROM FOUR TO LIKE ONE**"* → *"**I need to feel that you're concerned about what it is that I'M concerned about**"* — paired with encouragement: *"**get up here and pitch hard. And for that, you win our respect.**"* ★★★★★ **Then he takes a turn**, opening with nothing that sounds like a pitch (*"**is there anything you could use external expertise on that you don't have in-house?**"*), surfaces a budgeted need neither pitcher found, and **qualifies rather than pounces** (*"**would that have any material impact — or is that just a matter of pride?**"*). ★★★★★ **Blair's reaction is the proof**: *"**I didn't even know I was being sold**"* · *"**in 60 seconds you had me: who is it? I'll hire them**"* · *"**I would pay more.**"* ★★★★★ **The closing rules**: *"**STATEMENTS TELL, QUESTIONS SELL**… **sell through curiosity**… **don't make assumptions. Don't pitch. Don't convince**… **people buy for their own reasons**… **SEEK FIRST TO UNDERSTAND. IF YOU'RE TELLING, YOU'RE NOT SELLING.**"* Plus a new **Kier McLaren** item: *"**salespeople are the easiest people to sell to.**"* ⚠️ **Attribution:** the homeschooled-children analogy is **a panel member's, not Chris's** |

| 2023-02-16 | ★★★★★ [From Pushy to Personable: 5 Steps to Sell w/ Empathy](2023-02-16-yt-bNgRrcIcaFY.md) | sales-clients; mindset; business | ★★★★★ **LANDMARK — THE SALES ACRONYM END TO END**: **S**erve · **A**sk · **L**isten · **E**mpathize · **S**ummarize. *"**We all have the wrong concept of what sales is**… notice — **in SALES, the word 'sales' is not in sales at all. It's not giving a big old presentation. It's not manipulative.**"* ★★★★★ **And the DEPENDENCY that makes it a system, not a list**: *"**if you can get number one right, ALL THE OTHER ONES FALL IN LINE. If you screw up this idea of servitude, THE QUESTIONS YOU ASK WILL BE DIFFERENT, HOW YOU LISTEN WILL BE DIFFERENT, YOU WON'T EMPATHIZE AT ALL.**"* **SERVE** = *"**temporarily put on hold your own needs and wants**"* (with the military framing and *"an element of SELF-SACRIFICE"*) → learn **where they failed before, what success means, what's at stake if they fail** — *"before you even begin to take any action whatsoever."* **ASK** = calibrated questions (**Chris Voss**), the **Socratic open** (**Kevin Daley**, *Socratic Selling*), *"**so what's on your mind**"* (**Michael Bungay Stanier**) → ★★★★★ *"**RESIST THE COMPULSION TO IMMEDIATELY SOLVE THE PROBLEM** — they've just presented what THEY THINK the problem is"* → *"**instead of you convincing them, THEY'RE GOING TO GET CLARITY.**" **LISTEN** = *"most people just **stay quiet long enough so that they can formulate what they want to say next**"*, against ★★★★★ **his own podcast method**: *"I might have a whole list of questions — **MOST OF THE TIME I NEVER EVEN LOOK AT THEM AGAIN**… you'll see **EIGHT PAGES written on both sides**, rabbit holes, things I circle… **I'M TRYING TO RELIVE THE EMOTION WITH THEM.**" **EMPATHIZE** = *"in order to get to the future we must go to the past first"* (Daley) → *"**tell me how you FAILED in the past**"* → ★★★★★ *"**it would behoove you to present a solution that addresses all of these concerns. THIS BECOMES YOUR MARKETING COPY.**" **SUMMARIZE** = ★★★★★ **the fullest close in the corpus**: *"here's what I heard you say — does that sound right?"* → **the hypothetical close** (*"if you've done your job, it should be a SMOOTH, EFFORTLESS YES"*) → **the pause question** pre-empting buyer's remorse (*"is there anything else that would give you pause?"* — because *"there's so much rapport building and **they feel so guilty to say no**"*) → ★★★★★ **hand them the problem**: *"**what might be a solution to that? And you ACTIVATE THEM… and then THEY DESIGN A SOLUTION WITH YOU**"* → *"**is this a DEAL KILLER?**" Plus ★★★★★ **the smash-and-grab test** answering *what if the honest answer isn't my service*: *"**how long do I want to play this game?** If you're committed for three to six months **you're going to ROB YOUR CLIENT**… **your mindset determines your actions, and your actions determine the relationships**"*; ★★★★★ *"**you're CREATING A MEMORY for a person. THAT is customer service**"* (Nordstrom, Zappos); *"**what is the point of having ethics, morality, if you don't ever act on them?**"*; and ★★★★★ **Blair Enns, a fourth dated credit**: *"**the proposal isn't the document that you send — IT'S THE WORDS YOU SAY. The document is just a RECEIPT for the words.**" ★★★★★ **BIOGRAPHY**: *"**I work with someone TODAY that I met when I was in my EARLY 20s, when they were a tiny EIGHT-PERSON company — and they're a 400-PERSON company today**"* ⚠️ unnamed. ⚠️ **Distinct from the 2020 six-step process — do NOT merge the two frameworks** |
| 2023-02-17 | ★★★ [The Laws of Creativity: whatever you think, think the opposite — w/ Joey Cofone](2023-02-17-yt-691Um--5S-8.md) | mindset; design-craft; business | ⚠️ Guest-dominated for the book (**the laws are Joey Cofone's**) — but it carries **two provenance items, which is what makes it worth keeping.** ★★★★★ **HE SHOWS HIS WORKING ON A LINE THAT CIRCULATES AS HIS**: *"**JAMES VICTORE** has this expression which I love — **which I CO-OPTED AND MODIFIED a little bit.** His thing is **'what makes you weird as a kid makes you a creative as an adult'** — that's a lot to say. **I just say: WHAT MAKES YOU WEIRD MAKES YOU WONDERFUL**"* → ⚠️ **the corpus must never present this as wholly his.** Set up by *"only about **two percent of adults survive as a creative person**… **we learned a long time ago that to get along we have to go along — and BEING WEIRD GETS US OSTRACIZED**"*, and *"**I do everything I can to push people to EMBRACE YOUR INNER WEIRD.**" ★★★★ **A FIFTH dated Blair Enns credit**: *"**Blair Enns told me: do your due diligence, write a good book — because YOU CAN'T UNPUBLISH A BOOK**"* ✅ traced to its origin at [2017-05-03](2017-05-03-yt-KtL02T5MonA.md). ★★★★ On ghostwriting, then immediately against himself: *"**it's more admirable when it's you and it's all you**… maybe there's some **shots fired**, because **I did write my own book — and I'm like: I CAN'T DO ALL THESE THINGS, I NEED A LOT OF HELP.**" ★★★★ His arc in one line: *"**somewhere along the way I found my confidence, I found my voice, and I LET MY INNER FREAK FLAG OUT — and I raise it proudly.**" ★★★ Plus interviewer craft: **"this is me playing amateur psychoanalyst"** as a speculation hedge, and him narrating why a story landed (*"**this story took an ABRUPT TURN**"*) |

| 2024-10-22 | ★★★★★ [Chris Do Immigrant Journey Interview w/ Arjun Dhingra](2024-10-22-yt-XOGrJDvWAbM.md) | mindset; business | ★★★★★ **A LANDMARK — Chris is the SUBJECT**, and it is the fullest first-person account in the corpus of what the refugee background left him with, **told without romance.** ★★★★★ *"**I'm a FIRST-generation immigrant, so I wasn't born here**… when you flee a country due to war, civil war, and you flee communism and start over, **there are CERTAIN TRAUMAS THAT ARE JUST INHERITED IN THE BODY — that I don't learn about until much later, after talking to a therapist — THAT MY PARENTS FEEL**… I can't imagine what it's like to **drop everything — your identity, your language, your culture** — and start over **where EVERYTHING IS STACKED AGAINST YOU. BUT THEY SHIELDED US FROM IT.**"* ✅ Trauma attributed to his **parents**, his awareness dated to therapy, their shielding credited. ★★★★★ **The grades standard at its sharpest**: *"**if you get an A, THAT'S CALLED AVERAGE**… the fear of God was put in me: **if you get a C, DON'T EVEN BOTHER COMING HOME**"* → and the comparison that lands it: *"my friends who are Caucasian… **'I got a C, I'm getting a new BMX bike'** — I'm like: **LIFE IS SO UNFAIR.** Here I am getting A's… **THERE'S NO REWARD. IT'S JUST LACK OF PUNISHMENT — is all that is.**" ★★★★★ **Hardship as inherited capability, without romance**: *"**in the moment IT DOESN'T FEEL LIKE IT'S A GIFT. But it actually is** — you learn to **ADAPT TO SURVIVE OUT OF NECESSITY**… that becomes **THE BASELINE**, so as you progress **IT SEEMS LIKE NOTHING IS EVER AS BAD AS IT USED TO BE.**" With a Buddhist framing (*"**living the existence is painful**… it's **THE STORY THAT WE TELL OURSELVES** that frames it good or bad"*) and dated to *"my early to mid twenties"* — *"**I try to REIN IN THE EMOTIONS, to be AS STOIC AS POSSIBLE.**" ★★★★★ **ATTACHMENT as the mechanism of being stuck**: *"**we are MORE AFRAID OF CHANGE AND THE UNKNOWN THAN WE ARE OF OUR PROBLEMS** — we'll **stick with the devil that we know**"* → the example: the childhood defence that *"**even though it didn't serve you, IT PROTECTED YOU**"* and is still carried by *"a captain of industry… **NOBODY'S PICKING A FIGHT WITH YOU ANYMORE. THAT WAS IN THE PAST**"* → ⚠️ credited to *"**MY BUSINESS MENTOR**" (**not named here**): *"**oftentimes WE ARE RESPONDING TO THINGS THAT ARE NOT HERE RIGHT NOW**"* → *"you have to **UNBURDEN yourself… so that you can have THE STRENGTH TO CARRY SOMETHING ELSE.**" ★★★★ **"BLAME GOES BOTH WAYS"** — applied to **credit as well as fault**. ⚠️ Two attribution cautions: the mentor is unnamed, and ***"do less but better"* is flagged BY HIM as uncited** (*"I forget who — I should be able to cite this quote"*). | 2025-10-16 | ★★★ [Reluctant Coach Wants To Build Personal Brand — Ep. 2 w/ Matt Essam](2025-10-16-yt-PZgRQpu61k4.md) | branding; business; mindset | ★★★ **LANDMARK — ✅✅ the second joint appearance; the exception is now a SERIES.** Where Ep. 1 argued *why*, Ep. 2 answers the harder question: **what even IS a personal brand, and how would I know if I had one?** ★★★★ **The four-criteria reputation-vs-brand test — the first in the corpus**: ① do people **outside your vertical** know you · ② **can you attach your name to a thing and add value to it**, outside what you already do (Cleon Peterson's ~\$800 sculpture × 240 pre-orders; Twenty One Pilots merch at \$30 on a \$5 shirt; ⚠️ the counter-example — *"1:1 coaching **is not printing money. He's literally working for it**"*) · ③ **you stand at the top of your vertical** (*"you're not just another graphic design person who's speaking — **you're THE graphic designer**"*) · ④ **can you jump verticals** (Ryan Reynolds, The Rock — *"**they're not selling acting**… they can sell anything at this point. **That's what a brand is**"*). Premised on the decoupling: *"**you could be the poorest person and have an amazing personal brand**… **we have to decouple these ideas**"*, and Neumeier's *"**when enough people come to a similar gut feeling about who you are, then you have a brand**"*. | 2025-10-23 | ★★★★★ [Why You Need Controversy When Building an Online Brand](2025-10-23-yt-eluwXdhzJ5k.md) | branding; content-strategy; business; mindset | ★★★★★ **LANDMARK — the fullest exposition of "pick a fight", and the one that answers the obvious objection to it.** Host: Mo. ★★★★★ **The category distinction that makes the phrase usable**: *"**Those are ideas. I want to fight in the realm of ideas and philosophy**"* — the fights named are worth-tied-to-time, branding "experts" teaching marketing, *"**The Futur is like private art school without the crippling debt**"*, gurus with no receipts, and trolls. ★★★★★ **The test**: *"if you're not feeling either **I'm all in on what Chris is saying or I'm all against it**, then you're not really finding a lane."* ★★★★★ **The tree** — one stout trunk (*"pricing, communications, personal branding"*) + branches that feed it: *"**what I don't want to do is have a multi-trunk tree where some wind blows and the whole thing falls over**"*, with the treehouse sequencing rule. ★★★★★ **Symbiotic vs. parasitic** (shark + sucker fish) as the test for every side interest — *"**it's when it distracts from that… when you're becoming a stranger to your own family**"*. ★★★★★ **The BLM episode**: he posted, the Black and brown community told him to stay out, **he agreed** — *"**I suck the oxygen out of the room for people who are needing it**"* → the standing rule *"this is not my lane"*. ★★★★★ **"It was a parasite"** — The Futur draining Blind in 2014, the coach's objection, *"**I don't even know what it is yet**"*, and the systems that made the bet survivable. Plus *"if you're writing to grow your business, **that's called marketing**"*; *"**you're using marketing in the disguise of thought leadership**"*; the 70–80% regurgitation guess; *"**I wasn't very good at hosting the content part**"*; ⚠️ the unverified Japan *ban-the-word-good* study; and ★★★★★ **his refusal to be a permission-giver** — *"**I believe that's a parasitic relationship, not a symbiotic one**"* |
| 2025-10-25 | ★★★★ [Your Writing Feels Flat Because You're Missing This](2025-10-25-yt-Z0wnvRTWYmo.md) | content-strategy; communication; branding | ★★★★ **A live line-by-line writing workshop** — four named rules the corpus lacked in written form. ★★★★★ **The establishing shot**: *"**there's no setting**… **I want you to think like a director. All films open with an establishing shot**… and then **a story is told in increasingly close-up shots. Wide vista, tighter, tighter, tighter.**"* ★★★★ **Tension before relief** — *"we're always building tension"*; *"**relief has to come very late. Because if you get the relief, I don't read the rest.**"* ★★★★ **Sensory detail over summary** — *"**those details take us right there**"*; *"it becomes a generic story of observation, versus **putting me in there and making me feel the sweat**"*; *"write a **long-ass story** with great details, then **edit the sucker down**"* (AI endorsed as **editor, not author**). ★★★★★ **"Art is: you have an emotion, and you create something that when somebody else sees it, has a shared emotion with you — having not spoken to you"** → *"**in every paragraph ask: what is the emotion? No emotion, there's no story.**"* ★★★★★ **"Start at the end"** (beef Wellington vs. soup) — plus the clearest account yet of **how he retrieves stories live on stage**: *"**I know how the story ends. Now I'm looking for a story beginning.**"* ⚠️ The participant's 1996 Portland story is **theirs, not his**. ⚠️ Eighth *"I don't care about schedules or posting times"* attestation |
| 2025-10-30 | ★★★★★ [I Messed Up… The Truth About Niching Down](2025-10-30-yt-3ikc89b-tkE.md) | business; branding; mindset; content-strategy | ★★★★★ **LANDMARK — a public self-correction.** Host: Mo. *"**For so many years, I've told all of you to be hyper-niche… and here I am doing a lot of different things for a lot of different people — and I've lost my way.**"* ★★★★ **The five ones** (credited to **Taki Moore**): one target market, one offering, one conversion method, one traffic source, for one year — with his own scorecard: *"**I'm clearly not doing the five ones**… people often are confused. **So what do you do again?**"* ★★★★★ **Pressure = force ÷ area** — *"push across a wall, the wall will not break. **But if you push the same amount of pressure through a pea-sized shape, you can punch a hole through a wall**… **reduce the area of focus and you create tremendous pressure**"* — focus as **leverage, not discipline**. ★★★★★ **The Blind 1995–97 sequence, biography-grade**: the generalist years, **the client who sent their own senior animator to supervise the work** (*"and I wasn't embarrassed — **wow, there's so much I don't know**"*), the decision to focus on motion design, and twenty years later *"**do we do print?!** … **I was so good at this other new thing that people forgot about the old thing.**"* ★★★★★ **Identity**: *"in order for us to become the person we're meant to be, **we have to let go of the person we were**"* — thank the old self, then retire it. ⚠️★★★★ **Seventh non-violent-language attestation** — *"**even the way you phrase it sounds so violent to me**"*; *"I don't look at burial as a process of mourning. **I've got guests coming by.**"* ★★★★★ **And he refuses the host's "F what everybody thinks"**: *"**I think everybody wants me to be me**… the vast audience who likes me is **rooting for me to be more me**"* — a direct persona guard. Plus the mercenary/runway pattern; the offer gap (*"**prior to recently, I got no offer**"*) → Content Lab; and ★★★★★ *"**inside this cold exterior, this stoic exterior, is still an emotional immigrant boy**."* ⚠️ A family member's name appears in the captions and is deliberately not recorded |

| 2025-11-13 | ★★★★★ [What ACTUALLY Makes People Buy?](2025-11-13-yt-SAwZqaJl2Lk.md) | pricing; branding; sales-clients; business | ★★★★★ **LANDMARK — the missing causal spine under years of "emotional value commands higher prices" material.** He stacks **Marty Neumeier's brand commitment ladder** (*The Brand Flip*) onto **Maslow**, flagging the synthesis himself: *"**I'm going to be like a conspiracy theorist here**."* The four rungs: **satisfaction** (*"you did what you said"*) → **delight** (*"there's a peak experience and you might start to recommend it"* — free word of mouth) → **engagement** (*"you belong as part of a community or a tribe"* → automatic repurchase) → **empowerment** = *"**personal growth. What brand helps you to grow?**"* (room: CrossFit, Tony Robbins; his own interjection: *"The Futur. Thank you"*; Nike Run Club — *"**they will take all your money and you'll be happy to give it to them**"*). ★★★★★ **The conclusion**: *"**when you sell a product or service that helps them achieve this, you will command more money**"* → *"**where are you selling in this?**"* ★★★★★ **Apple vs. Dell, and he is fair to Dell**: *"**do you know how hard it is to find out how much something costs from Apple?**… **Go on the Dell website. Where's the price? It's right there on the front page — because they got nothing. They're not selling you a story. They're not helping you achieve status**"* — but *"**I just want it to perform, I want it to be cheap, I want it to be here tomorrow. Dell does a good job with that.**"* Plus *"**what you buy says something about you**… **even when you don't want to buy, that says something about you too**"* (his dad, Costco pants, the \$1.50 hot dog); *"**we vote with our money**"* → fast fashion; **Think Different** (Jobs, Chiat\\Day, Lee Clow) and ⚠️ **a contested Kerouac attribution he hedges himself**; and ★★★★ **"success leaves clues"** (Jim Rohn) turned against his own business model: *"**never have to pay another dollar in tuition, or courses, or master classes, or boot camps like this**"* — said to a paying room |
| 2025-11-15 | ★★★★★ [Why No One Watches YOUR Content (Live Coaching)](2025-11-15-yt-VkrkF0N-ZdE.md) | content-strategy; communication; sales-clients; branding | ★★★★★ **LANDMARK — the most complete live coaching session in the corpus**, with a business strategist (25 years a structural engineer) in the Content Lab hot seat. ★★★★★ **His discovery epistemology, narrated mid-session**: *"**whenever the prospect says something, I never assume them to tell the truth. You screw over everybody, including yourself.**"* → then he performs the story live in her voice and lands on *"**and have betrayed no one.**"* ★★★★★ **"It's a freaking lonely endeavor"** — he **hates recording alone**: *"**it sucks to stare into a piece of glass and say whatever**… **I can have 35 videos I need to make**… **cuz I hate doing it**"* — but with Drigo, Rich or Mo in the room, *"let's go make some content."* ⚠️ This complicates the *"when I feel inspired, I write"* self-image in the same video's outro. ★★★★ **Serve the snack, not the meal**: *"**no one's looking for the full meal**… you're delivering them **a steak dinner with a side of asparagus and probably some charred Brussels sprouts**"* — plus his own confessed version of the mistake, the **curiosity-gap split**, *"**we would sit here and write 100 hooks**"*, **the danger of smart people**, *"**the engineering brain is speaking, not the entertainer-educator**"*, and ★★★★★ *"**when the content starts working for you, you will be so happy to work on the content**… **if no one's looking at your content — content freaking sucks.**"* ⚠️ **Content Lab priced**: \$500/mo, \$5,000/yr, twice-monthly hot seats (⚠️ contradicts 2025-11-08's *weekly*) |

You've hired sales reps in the past and it costs a lot of money**… **whatever results you get, there's no transparency**… **Am I right?**"*) → *"**at that point I was already in. In fact, you've done it so many times you already know what I'm going to say. How's that for building rapport and trust?**"* ★★★★★ **The price-anchoring script**, dictated verbatim to a coaching client losing every job to a cheaper copycat — *"**you know whose problem this is? It's your problem**"* → *"**I'm going to be two to three times what anybody else will charge you**… **only call me if you don't feel confident that they're going to do a good job for you**"* + arm the buyer: *"**what evidence do you have that what you're saying has worked?**"* ✅ And it **fails first** — *"Chris, I just said goodbye to another client. Thanks a lot"* — before the client calls back three days later. ★★★★★ **"Do not explain"**: *"**speak confidently. Hold yourself high**… **whenever possible, do not explain anything. Try not to prove anything**"*; save proof as *"**your ammunition**"*; and the replacements — *"**that's what it costs**"* / *"**that's what it costs to work with people like me**"* / *"**policy.
